{"parentUuid":"10497135-2cd2-4a14-97e4-b4d73a4ae1b6","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"agent_listing_delta","addedTypes":["claude","claude-code-guide","Explore","general-purpose","Plan","statusline-setup"],"addedLines":["- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. (Tools: *)","- claude-code-guide: Use this agent when the user asks questions (\"Can Claude...\", \"Does Claude...\", \"How do I...\") about: (1) Claude Code (the CLI tool) - features, hooks, slash commands, MCP servers, settings, IDE integrations, keyboard shortcuts; (2) Claude Agent SDK - building custom agents; (3) Claude API (formerly Anthropic API) - Messages API for directly passing messages to Claude, Tool Runner (`client.beta.messages.tool_runner`) for running an agentic loop over your own tools, manual tool-use loops, Managed Agents for server-hosted agents with a managed sandbox, prompt caching, and general Anthropic SDK usage; (4) Claude Tag (Claude in Slack) - what it is, setting it up for a Slack workspace, `/install-slack-app`; (5) `claude plugin eval` (writing and running plugin eval suites, its JSON/report, sandbox, CI) and the `/skill-doctor` report. **IMPORTANT:** Before spawning a new agent, check if there is already a running or recently completed claude-code-guide agent that you can continue via SendMessage. (Tools: Bash, Read, WebFetch, WebSearch)","- Explore: Read-only search agent for broad fan-out searches — when answering means sweeping many files, directories, or naming conventions and you only need the conclusion, not the file dumps. It reads excerpts rather than whole files, so it locates code; it doesn't review or audit it. Specify search breadth: \"medium\" for moderate exploration, \"very thorough\" for multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, Artifact, ArtifactComments, ArtifactData, ArtifactCheck, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)","- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks. When you are searching for a keyword or file and are not confident that you will find the right match in the first few tries use this agent to perform the search for you. (Tools: *)","- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task. Returns step-by-step plans, identifies critical files, and considers architectural trade-offs. (Tools: All tools except Agent, Artifact, ArtifactComments, ArtifactData, ArtifactCheck, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)","- statusline-setup: Use this agent to configure the user's Claude Code status line setting. (Tools: Read, Edit)"],"builtInTypes":["claude","claude-code-guide","Explore","general-purpose","Plan","statusline-setup"],"builtInPluginTypes":[],"removedTypes":[],"isInitial":true,"showConcurrencyNote":false},"type":"attachment","uuid":"2e170899-abe8-4256-8831-aa5cf539b721","timestamp":"2026-10-21T19:31:55.994Z","rendered":[{"content":"<system-reminder>\nAvailable agent types for the Agent tool:\n- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. FleetView's default when no agent name is typed. (Tools: *)\n- claude-code-guide: Use this agent when the user asks questions (\"Can Claude...\", \"Does Claude...\", \"How do I...\") about: (1) Claude Code (the CLI tool) - features, hooks, slash commands, MCP servers, settings, IDE integrations, keyboard shortcuts; (2) Claude Agent SDK - building custom agents; (3) Claude API (formerly Anthropic API) - Messages API for directly passing messages to Claude, Tool Runner (`client.beta.messages.tool_runner`) for running an agentic loop over your own tools, manual tool-use loops, Managed Agents for server-hosted agents with a managed sandbox, prompt caching, and general Anthropic SDK usage; (4) Claude Tag (Claude in Slack) - what it is, setting it up for a Slack workspace, `/install-slack-app`; (5) `claude plugin eval` (writing and running plugin eval suites, its JSON/report, sandbox, CI) and the `/skill-doctor` report. **IMPORTANT:** Before spawning a new agent, check if there is already a running or recently completed claude-code-guide agent that you can continue via SendMessage. (Tools: Bash, Read, WebFetch, WebSearch)\n- Explore: Read-only search agent for broad fan-out searches — when answering means sweeping many files, directories, or naming conventions and you only need the conclusion, not the file dumps. It reads excerpts rather than whole files, so it locates code; it doesn't review or audit it. Specify search breadth: \"medium\" for moderate exploration, \"very thorough\" for multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, Artifact, ArtifactComments, ArtifactData, ArtifactCheck, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)\n- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks. When you are searching for a keyword or file and are not confident that you will find the right match in the first few tries use this agent to perform the search for you. (Tools: *)\n- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task. Returns step-by-step plans, identifies critical files, and considers architectural trade-offs. (Tools: All tools except Agent, Artifact, ArtifactComments, ArtifactData, ArtifactCheck, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)\n- statusline-setup: Use this agent to configure the user's Claude Code status line setting. (Tools: Read, Edit)\n</system-reminder>"}],"renderedRole":"system","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/biswas1/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"79ce639f-d0fe-42ed-9b5f-a887bcf43513","version":"2.1.306","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"2e170899-abe8-4256-8831-aa5cf539b721","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"mcp_instructions_delta","addedNames":["claude.ai Claude Docs"],"addedBlocks":["## claude.ai Claude Docs\nClaude Docs: living docs you create and edit here. A docs skill your client lists → load it before any docs call — also before a `read`, comment or tab change on a claude.ai …/artifact/… link (the link is a doc; never web-fetch it). No docs skill or guide text loaded → `guide( items = [\"topic.index\"] )` alone before any docs call but a doc's birth. Make a doc here — not a local file, even when coding — only when the user asks for one, and make it FIRST: the turn's first tool call is its skeleton (title, byline, a `pending` block per section) — a reflex: send it before any search, file read, plan, `guide` or thinking it through; think once it is open — `batch( container = {\"kind\":\"project\",\"create\":{\"name\":\"<title>\",\"doc\":{\"blocks\":{\"asof\":{\"type\":\"date\",\"value\":\"<today>\"},\"me\":{\"type\":\"mention\",\"user\":\"me\"},\"s1\":{\"type\":\"pending\",\"intent\":\"Goals: the three outcomes this quarter commits to\"},\"s2\":{…}},\"markdown\":\"# <title>\\n\\n<?claude block asof?> · <?claude block me?>\\n\\n<?claude block s1?>\\n\\n<?claude block s2?>\"}}}, batch = [] )` (`<?claude block k?>` ↔ `blocks.k`); its ack links the doc → `open` it with your Artifact tool (none → start your next message with the link, once); they're likely watching it fill — keep them posted in a short line naming what you're on (outline up; now <topic>); findings go in the doc, not chat; then `guide( items = [\"topic.index\"] )`, research, and fill each section: `replace` its pending id with `## <heading>` + body; end with one line + the link, never the document. Summoned by a doc comment (turn headed `[Artifact comment sent to Claude]`, `;thread=<root id>`): answer ONLY with a doc comment under that root (`create` an utterance, parent `<root id>`) — no artifact/platform comment tool: that relay thread is resolved and never reaches the doc; an edit asked there → `update` with `answering: \"<root id>\"`."],"removedNames":[]},"type":"attachment","uuid":"2d4dd98f-73fa-4165-8a18-5a5b22a6d3ef","timestamp":"2026-10-21T19:31:55.994Z","rendered":[{"content":"<system-reminder>\n# MCP Server Instructions\n\nThe following MCP servers have provided instructions for how to use their tools and resources:\n\n## claude.ai Claude Docs\nClaude Docs: living docs you create and edit here. A docs skill your client lists → load it before any docs call — also before a `read`, comment or tab change on a claude.ai …/artifact/… link (the link is a doc; never web-fetch it). No docs skill or guide text loaded → `guide( items = [\"topic.index\"] )` alone before any docs call but a doc's birth. Make a doc here — not a local file, even when coding — only when the user asks for one, and make it FIRST: the turn's first tool call is its skeleton (title, byline, a `pending` block per section) — a reflex: send it before any search, file read, plan, `guide` or thinking it through; think once it is open — `batch( container = {\"kind\":\"project\",\"create\":{\"name\":\"<title>\",\"doc\":{\"blocks\":{\"asof\":{\"type\":\"date\",\"value\":\"<today>\"},\"me\":{\"type\":\"mention\",\"user\":\"me\"},\"s1\":{\"type\":\"pending\",\"intent\":\"Goals: the three outcomes this quarter commits to\"},\"s2\":{…}},\"markdown\":\"# <title>\\n\\n<?claude block asof?> · <?claude block me?>\\n\\n<?claude block s1?>\\n\\n<?claude block s2?>\"}}}, batch = [] )` (`<?claude block k?>` ↔ `blocks.k`); its ack links the doc → `open` it with your Artifact tool (none → start your next message with the link, once); they're likely watching it fill — keep them posted in a short line naming what you're on (outline up; now <topic>); findings go in the doc, not chat; then `guide( items = [\"topic.index\"] )`, research, and fill each section: `replace` its pending id with `## <heading>` + body; end with one line + the link, never the document. Summoned by a doc comment (turn headed `[Artifact comment sent to Claude]`, `;thread=<root id>`): answer ONLY with a doc comment under that root (`create` an utterance, parent `<root id>`) — no artifact/platform comment tool: that relay thread is resolved and never reaches the doc; an edit asked there → `update` with `answering: \"<root id>\"`.\n</system-reminder>"}],"renderedRole":"system","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/biswas1/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"79ce639f-d0fe-42ed-9b5f-a887bcf43513","version":"2.1.306","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"2d4dd98f-73fa-4165-8a18-5a5b22a6d3ef","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- find-skills: Helps users discover and install agent skills when they ask questions like \"how do I do X\", \"find a skill for X\", \"is there a skill that can...\", or express interest in extending capabilities. This skill should be used when the user is looking for functionality that might exist as an installable skill.\n- code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes: Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/spec asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\n- diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says \"diagnose\"/\"debug this\", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.\n- domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when discussing codebase terminology, writing or editing a CONTEXT.md, or recording or editing an ADR.\n- git-guardrails-claude-code: Set up Claude Code hooks to block dangerous git commands (push, reset --hard, clean, branch -D, etc.) before they execute. Use when user wants to prevent destructive git operations, add git safety hooks, or block git push/reset in Claude Code.\n- grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- migrate-to-shoehorn: Migrate test files from `as` type assertions to @total-typescript/shoehorn. Use when user mentions shoehorn, wants to replace `as` in tests, or needs partial test data.\n- pr: Use when writing a PR body.\n- prototype: Build a throwaway prototype to answer a design question. Use when the user wants to sanity-check whether a state model or logic feels right, or explore what a UI should look like.\n- research: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\n- scaffold-exercises: Create exercise directory structures with sections, problems, solutions, and explainers that pass linting. Use when user wants to scaffold exercises, create exercise stubs, or set up a new course section.\n- setup-pre-commit: Set up Husky pre-commit hooks with lint-staged (Prettier), type checking, and tests in the current repo. Use when user wants to add pre-commit hooks, set up Husky, configure lint-staged, or add commit-time formatting/typechecking/testing.\n- tdd: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to build features or fix bugs test-first, mentions \"red-green-refactor\", or wants integration tests.\n- wizard: Generate an interactive bash wizard that walks a human through steps only they can perform. Use when provisioning infrastructure, setting up credentials or CI secrets, walking an unfamiliar third-party dashboard, or running a one-off migration or cutover. Don't invoke this for steps the agent can perform itself.\n- writing-for-agents: Writing documents for agents. Use when creating or editing skills, or modifying AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md.\n- mattpocock-skills:diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says \"diagnose\"/\"debug this\", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.\n- mattpocock-skills:tdd: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to sanity-check whether a state model or logic feels right, or explore what a UI should look like.\n- mattpocock-skills:research: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\n- mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when discussing codebase terminology, writing or editing a CONTEXT.md, or recording or editing an ADR.\n- mattpocock-skills:codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\n- mattpocock-skills:code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes: Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/spec asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\n- mattpocock-skills:wizard: Generate an interactive bash wizard that walks a human through steps only they can perform. Use when provisioning infrastructure, setting up credentials or CI secrets, walking an unfamiliar third-party dashboard, or running a one-off migration or cutover. Don't invoke this for steps the agent can perform itself.\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- mattpocock-skills:writing-for-agents: Writing documents for agents. Use when creating or editing skills, or modifying AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts. - Load before writing any artifact, including a skill-instructed Markdown one - Markdown is never a shortcut past the design pass.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts - when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, remembering what people do on it (a poll, a sign-up sheet, a checklist, a document edited in place — it saves new versions of itself), keeping state shared across viewers, knowing who is viewing, asking Claude a question of its own, storing files people add, handing the viewer a file to save, or using the viewer's camera, microphone, location, screen or device motion. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever any such runtime behavior would make an artifact more useful, before writing the page.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- plugin-authoring: Make a mod: a change to Claude's own interface or behaviour, such as a panel or pane that stays beside the chat and updates, a band, status line, toast, slash command, or hook on tool calls. Use to make, change or debug a mod or its hooks module.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch\n- anthropic-skills:built-in-browser: Read this skill before the first step that uses the built-in browser, the browser pane inside the Claude desktop app (also called the in-app browser, the browser pane, Claude's browser, or \"your own browser\"), whose tools are named mcp__Claude_Browser__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use.\n- anthropic-skills:chrome-browser: Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use.\n- anthropic-skills:computer-use: Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills.\n- anthropic-skills:deep-research: Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly.\n- anthropic-skills:docs: docs (editable docs people share and comment on; the default for any document, named as a doc or not: a document, report, proposal, resume, cover letter, letter, contract, policy, form, template, worksheet, essay, handbook, guide, how-to, cheat sheet, SOP or other writing to keep, share, collaborate on, send, submit, print or sign; a doc exports to Word, PDF, Markdown or Google Docs, so needing a file to send, attach, upload, submit or print is no reason to pick Word, and a file nobody asked for is a doc, not Word; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat; a pasted claude.ai artifact link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; Word or another file format named, tracked changes wanted, or a .docx to change or use as a template → that format's skill): making one → if no docs-connector instructions are in context, call its `guide` (topic.instructions) first; then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of Microsoft Word Documents, such as 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', 'microsoft doc'. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead, even if they will email or print it. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:google-workspace: Read this before the first Google Drive, Docs, Sheets or Slides connector call whenever the task creates or changes a Google file. Use this skill whenever the user wants to create or change a Google Doc, Sheet or Slides file in their Google Drive. Triggers include: a request that names Google Docs, Sheets, Slides or Drive and asks to make, edit, format, copy or rename a file; a docs.google.com link with a request to change that file, even a one-line fix or suggested edits; and any follow-up change to a Google file from earlier in the chat, even \"change it\" or \"add a tab\". Includes helper scripts for document positions, cell ranges and slide layout. However, if the user asks for a doc, deck or spreadsheet without naming Google, or gives a Google file only as source material for something new, use Claude's own output type instead. Do NOT use for read-only questions about a Google file, or for Word, Excel, PowerPoint or PDF files.\n- anthropic-skills:import-memory: Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. {"parentUuid":"10deb4ee-2ee9-4173-a311-02cbe60b8fde","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"instructions","files":[{"path":"/Users/biswas1/.claude/CLAUDE.md","type":"User","content":"# CLAUDE.md\n\nThis file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.\n\n## Project Overview\n\nThis repository builds a static course website (Kenyon CS-style educational site). The site is assembled from source files (HTML, CSS, markdown/templates) into a `dist/` output directory, then served locally or deployed to GitHub Pages. The build process must be deterministic and reproducible.\n\n## Working Style\n\n- **Action over explanation**: When a request could mean \"explain\" or \"do\", execute the action immediately. Default to performing the work rather than stopping after an explanation.\n- **Status transparency**: For multi-step setup or build tasks, always print a brief checklist (DONE/SKIPPED/BLOCKED status) before ending the turn so the user can see what completed.\n- **Preflight checks for remote ops**: Before any `git push` or `gh` command that affects the remote repository, run `gh auth status` first. If auth fails, stop and report the error rather than attempting the operation. Never skip or suppress this check.\n\n## Build & Development Commands\n\nWhen the build system is set up, use these patterns:\n\n- **Build the site**: `./build.sh` or equivalent (to be determined once source structure is established)\n- **Preview locally**: `python3 -m http.server 8000` from the `dist/` directory, then visit `http://localhost:8000`\n- **Run tests** (when added): `pytest` or equivalent test runner\n- **Run a single test**: `pytest tests/test_name.py::test_function`\n- **Lint/format checks**: `ruff check .` (or similar per project setup)\n\n## Web / Static Site Rules\n\nAfter any routing, navigation, or file-movement change:\n\n1. **Serve and verify locally**: Build the output, then serve it locally with `python3 -m http.server` and curl/check every navigation link and page load.\n2. **Assert no regressions**: Diff the before/after word count and key headings of each page to confirm no content was silently dropped during refactoring.\n3. **Test hash-based routing** (if applicable): For pages that use hash routing (e.g., `#week-2`), verify that:\n   - Direct navigation to `http://localhost:8000/page#week-2` loads the correct section\n   - Links to the hash work when clicked from within the page\n4. Never commit or push a build that has broken or missing internal links — always verify the built output before pushing.\n\n## Task Completion & Progress Tracking\n\nFor multi-step work (setup, refactor, deploy):\n\n- Maintain a `PROJECT_STATE.md` file listing each step with status (DONE/PENDING/BLOCKED), current toolchain versions, and known issues.\n- Update `PROJECT_STATE.md` immediately after each step completes.\n- Always re-read `PROJECT_STATE.md` at the start of a new session to avoid re-doing completed work.\n\n## Git & Deployment\n\n- **Verify credentials first**: Always run `gh auth status` before any push or GitHub interaction. If authentication is missing or expired, stop and report rather than retrying.\n- **Idempotent setup**: Ensure any setup scripts (e.g., `setup.sh`) can be run multiple times safely without side effects or re-installing already-present tools.\n- **GitHub Pages deployment**: When deploying to GitHub Pages, verify that:\n  - The site builds and all links resolve locally before pushing\n  - GitHub Pages is configured to deploy from the correct branch/directory (usually `gh-pages` or `/dist`)\n  - The published site is reachable and displays correctly\n\n## Code Architecture (To Be Updated)\n\nAs the project develops:\n\n- **Source directory**: TBD (will likely be `src/` for templates/components)\n- **Build script**: TBD (Python script or shell script that assembles source into `dist/`)\n- **Test suite**: TBD (end-to-end tests using Playwright or similar to crawl and verify all routes)\n- **Deployment**: GitHub Actions or manual `gh pages` deployment\n\n## Notes for Future Sessions\n\n- Large refactors that touch multiple files (100+ edits) often introduce subtle regressions (routing mismatches, dropped content). Always run the verification checklist after large changes, not just before.\n- If you encounter a broken link or missing content in the bundled preview, diff the source files *and* the built HTML to identify where the content disappeared.\n- For performance: prioritize user feedback over automation — broken navigation is worse than no link verification, so always let the user know when verification fails."},{"path":"/Users/biswas1/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/CLAUDE.md","type":"Project","content":"# CLAUDE.md — standing instructions\n\nKeep this file short. It is prepended to every turn, so every line costs context\non every message. Add a rule only after you have had to correct the same thing\ntwice.\n\n## What this repo is\n\nA small web CMS for IPHS 400 Mini-Project #2. A local admin console\n(FastAPI + Jinja + SQLite) writes content; `cms publish` renders the published\ncontent into `site/` as static HTML, which is deployed to GitHub Pages. The\nadmin console never goes on the public internet.\n\n## Hard constraints\n\n- Published HTML uses **relative** paths only. Never `href=\"/...\"` or `src=\"/...\"`.\n- Never commit `.env`, `*.db`, keys, or tokens. Read secrets from the environment.\n- Passwords are hashed with argon2. Never store or log a plain password.\n- Every state-changing form carries a CSRF token.\n- User-written Markdown is sanitized before it is rendered anywhere.\n- `site/` is generated. Never edit it by hand.\n- Only published content reaches `site/`. Drafts never leave the database.\n\n## Course rules\n\n- The project glossary is CONTEXT.md (never GLOSSARY.md). /grill-with-docs and every other skill must read and write CONTEXT.md.\n- Required workflow for this course: /grill-with-docs -> /to-spec -> /to-tickets (7-9 tickets) -> /implement one ticket per fresh session -> /code-review. Do not use /implement-spec or /pr.\n- Work directly on main; each ticket closes with a commit starting \"T0N:\" and ending \"Closes #N\".\n\n## How to work with me\n\n- One ticket per session. Read the ticket issue, the spec issue, and `CONTEXT.md`\n  before writing code.\n- Write the ticket id into `.claude/state/phase` when a stage starts\n  (`grill`, `spec`, `tickets`, or `T03`), so the usage ledger is labelled.\n- Use the vocabulary in `CONTEXT.md`. If a better word appears, update the\n  glossary rather than using both.\n- When `/status` shows a non-Anthropic base URL, add a `Backend: <provider> <model>`\n  trailer to every commit made in that session.\n- Before a commit that closes a ticket: post the `/code-review` findings, and how\n  each was resolved, as a comment on that issue. Then commit with a message that\n  starts `T0N:` and ends `Closes #N`.\n- Ask before adding a dependency. The stack in `pyproject.toml` is fixed for this\n  project.\n\n## Agent skills\n\n### Issue tracker\n\nWork is tracked in GitHub Issues. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\n\n### Triage labels\n\nIssues use five canonical labels: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\n\n### Domain docs\n\nSingle-context glossary and ADRs. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\n\n## Long sessions (shared standard)\n- Session handoffs are dated, write-once files in `docs/handoffs/`, named `<YYYY-MM-DD_HHMM>_<slug>-claude.md`: `/handoff <slug>` writes one, `/handoff-load <slug>` loads one by name. `LATEST*.md` and `INDEX.md` are retired stubs (2026-10-06): never read, write or import them. Codex keeps its own records in `docs/handoffs/codex/`.\n- Compaction snapshots and the automatic compaction record come from the global `compact_snapshot.py` hook (global CLAUDE.md, \"Long runs\" and \"Compaction\"); this repository has no handoff hooks of its own.\n- Session exports (`/export` files, `codex-session-*.md`) and other files that appear in this repository from another session, agent, directory or machine are the owner's work, never foreign. Exports live in `transcripts/` (move a stray one there), are scanned by the git hooks, and are included in every commit; `bin/repo-standardize` in `~/code/ai-swe-setup` does the move.\n- Decisions and gotchas discovered in conversation go into the handoff or an ADR under `docs/adr/`, never only in chat.\n- When compacting, preserve: the goal, the ordered next steps, every decision with its reason, the modified files and their state, the exact test and lint commands with their last results, and the open questions. Drop raw tool output and resolved detours."}]},"type":"attachment","uuid":"ffbf6f19-dcd8-4381-b5ee-55e2df81fc4b","timestamp":"2026-10-21T19:31:56.144Z","rendered":[{"content":"<system-reminder>\nCodebase and user instructions are shown below. Default to performing the work rather than stopping after an explanation.\n- **Status transparency**: For multi-step setup or build tasks, always print a brief checklist (DONE/SKIPPED/BLOCKED status) before ending the turn so the user can see what completed.\n- **Preflight checks for remote ops**: Before any `git push` or `gh` command that affects the remote repository, run `gh auth status` first. If auth fails, stop and report the error rather than attempting the operation. Never skip or suppress this check.\n\n## Build & Development Commands\n\nWhen the build system is set up, use these patterns:\n\n- **Build the site**: `./build.sh` or equivalent (to be determined once source structure is established)\n- **Preview locally**: `python3 -m http.server 8000` from the `dist/` directory, then visit `http://localhost:8000`\n- **Run tests** (when added): `pytest` or equivalent test runner\n- **Run a single test**: `pytest tests/test_name.py::test_function`\n- **Lint/format checks**: `ruff check .` (or similar per project setup)\n\n## Web / Static Site Rules\n\nAfter any routing, navigation, or file-movement change:\n\n1. **Serve and verify locally**: Build the output, then serve it locally with `python3 -m http.server` and curl/check every navigation link and page load.\n2. **Assert no regressions**: Diff the before/after word count and key headings of each page to confirm no content was silently dropped during refactoring.\n3. **Test hash-based routing** (if applicable): For pages that use hash routing (e.g., `#week-2`), verify that:\n   - Direct navigation to `http://localhost:8000/page#week-2` loads the correct section\n   - Links to the hash work when clicked from within the page\n4. Never commit or push a build that has broken or missing internal links — always verify the built output before pushing.\n\n## Task Completion & Progress Tracking\n\nFor multi-step work (setup, refactor, deploy):\n\n- Maintain a `PROJECT_STATE.md` file listing each step with status (DONE/PENDING/BLOCKED), current toolchain versions, and known issues.\n- Update `PROJECT_STATE.md` immediately after each step completes.\n- Always re-read `PROJECT_STATE.md` at the start of a new session to avoid re-doing completed work.\n\n## Git & Deployment\n\n- **Verify credentials first**: Always run `gh auth status` before any push or GitHub interaction. If authentication is missing or expired, stop and report rather than retrying.\n- **Idempotent setup**: Ensure any setup scripts (e.g., `setup.sh`) can be run multiple times safely without side effects or re-installing already-present tools.\n- **GitHub Pages deployment**: When deploying to GitHub Pages, verify that:\n  - The site builds and all links resolve locally before pushing\n  - GitHub Pages is configured to deploy from the correct branch/directory (usually `gh-pages` or `/dist`)\n  - The published site is reachable and displays correctly\n\n## Code Architecture (To Be Updated)\n\nAs the project develops:\n\n- **Source directory**: TBD (will likely be `src/` for templates/components)\n- **Build script**: TBD (Python script or shell script that assembles source into `dist/`)\n- **Test suite**: TBD (end-to-end tests using Playwright or similar to crawl and verify all routes)\n- **Deployment**: GitHub Actions or manual `gh pages` deployment\n\n## Notes for Future Sessions\n\n- Large refactors that touch multiple files (100+ edits) often introduce subtle regressions (routing mismatches, dropped content). Always run the verification checklist after large changes, not just before.\n- If you encounter a broken link or missing content in the bundled preview, diff the source files *and* the built HTML to identify where the content disappeared.\n- For performance: prioritize user feedback over automation — broken navigation is worse than no link verification, so always let the user know when verification fails.\n\nContents of /Users/biswas1/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/CLAUDE.md (project instructions, checked into the codebase):\n\n# CLAUDE.md — standing instructions\n\nKeep this file short. It is prepended to every turn, so every line costs context\non every message. Add a rule only after you have had to correct the same thing\ntwice.\n\n## What this repo is\n\nA small web CMS for IPHS 400 Mini-Project #2. A local admin console\n(FastAPI + Jinja + SQLite) writes content; `cms publish` renders the published\ncontent into `site/` as static HTML, which is deployed to GitHub Pages. The\nadmin console never goes on the public internet.\n\n## Hard constraints\n\n- Published HTML uses **relative** paths only. Never `href=\"/...\"` or `src=\"/...\"`.\n- Never commit `.env`, `*.db`, keys, or tokens. Read secrets from the environment.\n- Passwords are hashed with argon2. Never store or log a plain password.\n- Every state-changing form carries a CSRF token.\n- User-written Markdown is sanitized before it is rendered anywhere.\n- `site/` is generated. Never edit it by hand.\n- Only published content reaches `site/`. Drafts never leave the database.\n\n## Course rules\n\n- The project glossary is CONTEXT.md (never GLOSSARY.md). /grill-with-docs and every other skill must read and write CONTEXT.md.\n- Required workflow for this course: /grill-with-docs -> /to-spec -> /to-tickets (7-9 tickets) -> /implement one ticket per fresh session -> /code-review. Do not use /implement-spec or /pr.\n- Work directly on main; each ticket closes with a commit starting \"T0N:\" and ending \"Closes #N\".\n\n## How to work with me\n\n- One ticket per session. Read the ticket issue, the spec issue, and `CONTEXT.md`\n  before writing code.\n- Write the ticket id into `.claude/state/phase` when a stage starts\n  (`grill`, `spec`, `tickets`, or `T03`), so the usage ledger is labelled.\n- Use the vocabulary in `CONTEXT.md`. If a better word appears, update the\n  glossary rather than using both.\n- When `/status` shows a non-Anthropic base URL, add a `Backend: <provider> <model>`\n  trailer to every commit made in that session.\n- Before a commit that closes a ticket: post the `/code-review` findings, and how\n  each was resolved, as a comment on that issue. Then commit with a message that\n  starts `T0N:` and ends `Closes #N`.\n- Ask before adding a dependency. The stack in `pyproject.toml` is fixed for this\n  project.\n\n## Agent skills\n\n### Issue tracker\n\nWork is tracked in GitHub Issues. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\n\n### Triage labels\n\nIssues use five canonical labels: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\n\n### Domain docs\n\nSingle-context glossary and ADRs. Codex keeps its own records in `docs/handoffs/codex/`.\n- Compaction snapshots and the automatic compaction record come from the global `compact_snapshot.py` hook (global CLAUDE.md, \"Long runs\" and \"Compaction\"); this repository has no handoff hooks of its own.\n- Session exports (`/export` files, `codex-session-*.md`) and other files that appear in this repository from another session, agent, directory or machine are the owner's work, never foreign. Exports live in `transcripts/` (move a stray one there), are scanned by the git hooks, and are included in every commit; `bin/repo-standardize` in `~/code/ai-swe-setup` does the move.\n- Decisions and gotchas discovered in conversation go into the handoff or an ADR under `docs/adr/`, never only in chat.\n- When compacting, preserve: the goal, the ordered next steps, every decision with its reason, the modified files and their state, the exact test and lint commands with their last results, and the open questions. {"parentUuid":"f1289e8b-a725-463f-a8f1-3127af1d4495","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"96c4a598-d813-44c0-b274-9def82d4af64","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UWcErvyXxKk1DJswj8Dhbi","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Mini-Project #2plus: Extend Your Web CMS\n2\t**Course:** IPHS 400: Frontiers in AI, Kenyon College\n3\t**Instructor:** Jon A. Chun · **Date:** 2026-10-12 (v3) · **Weeks 8–9** · **Extra credit**\n4\t**Changes:** v3: 15 exercises at 5–10 points, 15–60 min each, +30 cap; live deploy removed; assigned Oct 11.\n5\t**Format:** Self-guided lab manual. A menu of optional exercises: pick any, in any order, at your own pace.\n6\t\n7\t---\n8\t\n9\t## Front matter\n10\t### What this is\n11\tMP2plus is a **menu of 15 independent exercises** that extend the CMS you built in MP2. Pick any number, in any order, and stop whenever you like. Your points are **added to your MP2 score**, up to **+30**.\n12\t\n13\tThe menu has three groups:\n14\t- **Features** (F1–F9) come from MP2's stretch list, which MP2 introduced but did not score.\n15\t- **Process** (P1–P4) covers habits MP2 introduced but did not require, or scored only lightly.\n16\t- **Security** (S1–S2) extends two lines of `SECURITY-CHECKLIST.md` that MP2 scored only lightly.\n17\t\n18\t### The menu\n19\t| Id | Exercise | Minutes | Pts |\n20\t|---|---|---|---|\n21\t| F1 | Media uploads with alt text | 45–60 | 9 |\n22\t| F2 | Categories and tags | 40–55 | 8 |\n23\t| F3 | Revision history with rollback | 40–55 | 8 |\n24\t| F4 | Scheduled publishing | 35–50 | 7 |\n25\t| F5 | Site search | 35–50 | 7 |\n26\t| F6 | Comments with moderation | 45–60 | 9 |\n27\t| F7 | Plugin or hook system | 45–60 | 9 |\n28\t| F8 | Switchable theme | 35–50 | 7 |\n29\t| F9 | Production configuration | 40–55 | 8 |\n30\t| P1 | Pull-request workflow | 30–45 | 6 |\n31\t| P2 | `/prototype` side session | 35–50 | 7 |\n32\t| P3 | Decision record | 30–45 | 6 |\n33\t| P4 | Budget re-forecast | 30–40 | 6 |\n34\t| S1 | Direct-URL access matrix | 30–45 | 6 |\n35\t| S2 | Login hardening | 40–55 | 8 |\n36\t| | **Menu total (up to 30 count)** | | **111** |\n37\t\n38\tPick what interests you, not everything. Reaching +30 takes three or four exercises, about three hours. One or two exercises is a solid MP2plus.\n39\t\n40\t### Time and deadlines\n41\t| Milestone | What | When (Eastern, EDT) |\n42\t|---|---|---|\n43\t| **Assigned** | This manual and the rubric | **Sun Oct 11, 2026** |\n44\t| **Due** | Tag `mp2plus-final` pushed, report emailed | **Tue Oct 20, 2026, 2:40 pm** |\n45\t| **Grace** | No penalty | until **Wed Oct 21, 2026, 2:40 pm** |\n46\t\n47\tAround the exercises you pick, two short parts are required and worth 0 points: Part 0, before you start (15 minutes), and Part 2, submit (20 minutes).\n48\t\n49\t### Prerequisites\n50\t- **Your MP2 repo**, `iphs400-mp2-cms`, with `mp2-final` pushed. The MP2 resubmission is due Thu Oct 15, 2:40 pm (no-penalty grace until Fri Oct 16, 2:40 pm); Part 0 says how the two overlap.\n51\t- **Your MP2 setup still working:** `uv`, `gh` logged in, Claude Code with the mattpocock skills (`/implement`, `/tdd`, `/code-review`, `/prototype`, `/handoff`), the hooks, and the usage ledger.\n52\t- **These two files on GitHub.** Your repo was copied before they existed. Read them in the starter repo, `jon-chun/iphs400-mp2-cms-starter`: this manual and `docs/mp2plus-grading-rubric_20261012.md`.\n53\t\n54\t> **Policy (set by the instructor).** MP2plus was assigned **Sun Oct 11, 2026**, after the first MP2 final submissions were graded, to give deeper hands-on practice and a way to close the gaps the MP2 rubric found. It is an optional menu of **15 independent exercises**. Each takes **15–60 minutes** and is worth **5–10 points** according to its time and difficulty: about 1 point per 6 minutes.\n55\t> **Points are added to the student's MP2 grade**, for every student, whatever the MP2 score. MP2 is graded as a numeric score out of 100. MP2plus points are added after the MP2 resubmission (due Thu Oct 15, 2026, 2:40 pm; no-penalty grace until Fri Oct 16, 2:40 pm) is graded. **Cap: +30 per student.** The sum may exceed 100 and is recorded as is: MP2 91 + MP2plus 24 = 115.\n56\t> **Due Tue Oct 20, 2026, 2:40 pm**; no-penalty grace until **Wed Oct 21, 2026, 2:40 pm**. One MP2plus resubmission within one week of feedback.\n57\t> **The gate.** An exercise earns points only if, at `mp2plus-final`, the MP2 checker (`check_submission.py --stage 2`) and `uv run pytest -q` show no failure that was absent at `mp2plus-base`. A failing test added for MP2plus costs only its own exercise's Tests point. Failures already present at `mp2plus-base` are reported, not scored, so MP2plus is not a repair pass. Solo work: every commit after `mp2plus-base` is authored by the student. No `mp2plus-base` tag, no points.\n58\t\n59\t### Deliverables checklist\n60\tTick every line that applies before you tag `mp2plus-final`.\n61\t\n62\t- [ ] Tag `mp2plus-base` pushed before your first MP2plus commit\n63\t- [ ] `notes/mp2plus-plan.md` committed: the exercises you intend, minute estimates, the base check results\n64\t- [ ] Each exercise you did: everything its **Deliverables** line names\n65\t- [ ] `docs/iphs400_mp2plus-web-cms_report_{first}-{last}_{YYYYMMDD}.md` with each exercise's **Report row**, committed before the tag\n66\t- [ ] No checker or test failure that was not there at `mp2plus-base`; tag `mp2plus-final` pushed; email sent\n67\t\n68\t---\n69\t\n70\t## Part 0 — Before you start\n71\t**Time: 15 min. No points, but required: without `mp2plus-base`, no exercise earns points.**\n72\t\n73\t### 0.1 Tag your baseline\n74\t`mp2plus-base` marks the commit graded for MP2. Everything after it is MP2plus, and the judge scores only that range. Run the two MP2 checks first:\n75\t\n76\t```bash\n77\tcd ~/code/iphs400-mp2-cms\n78\tgit switch main && git pull\n79\tuv run python scripts/check_submission.py --stage 2\n80\tuv run pytest -q\n81\t```\n82\t\n83\t**Expected:** the checker ends with `N/N checks passed.` and pytest ends with `passed`. A `FAIL` line or a failing test here is a pre-existing failure. Copy it into your plan (0.2). It is reported, not scored, and you need not fix it for MP2plus. Then tag the commit graded for MP2:\n84\t\n85\t```bash\n86\tgit tag mp2plus-base mp2-final\n87\tgit push origin mp2plus-base\n88\t```\n89\t\n90\t**Expected:** `* [new tag] mp2plus-base -> mp2plus-base`.\n91\t\n92\t- **Resubmitting MP2 by Oct 15?** The simplest order is to push your revised `mp2-final` first, then run the two commands above.\n93\t- **Starting MP2plus before you resubmit?** Tag now. Make the MP2 fixes on a branch from `mp2-final` (`git switch -c mp2-resubmit mp2-final`), move the tag there (`git tag -f mp2-final && git push -f origin mp2-final`), and `git switch main` to continue MP2plus. Before you tag `mp2plus-final`, run `git merge mp2-resubmit` on `main` and name the merge commit in your report. Those commits are MP2 work, and MP2plus does not score them.\n94\t\n95\t### 0.2 Write your plan\n96\tSkim the menu and list what you intend to do, with minute estimates from the menu. The plan may change; your report says what you actually did. Create `notes/mp2plus-plan.md`, then commit it on `main` with the message `mp2plus: plan` and push:\n97\t\n98\t```text\n99\t# MP2plus plan\n100\tExercises: F2 about 50 min; S2 about 50 min; P4 about 35 min\n101\tAt mp2plus-base: checker \"<its last line>\", FAIL lines: <names, or none>; pytest \"<its last line>\"\n102\t```\n103\t\n104\tIf you picked P4, its \"before\" run goes into this file now (see P4).\n105\t\n106\t> **The gate.** At `mp2plus-final` the judge runs the same two checks. A check or a test that passed at `mp2plus-base` and fails at `mp2plus-final` sets every exercise to 0 until a resubmission fixes it. Test files that existed at `mp2plus-base` may change only by adding cases. If an exercise truly needs an existing assertion changed, say so in that exercise's findings comment and its report row; the judge then reviews `git diff mp2plus-base..mp2plus-final` for those files.\n107\t\n108\t---\n109\t\n110\t## Part 1 — The menu\n111\t- **Pick any.** The exercises are independent. P1 carries one other change, P2 needs code where its decision lands, and P4 measures one other exercise; each says so under **Prerequisites**.\n112\t- **New work only.** Only commits after `mp2plus-base` count. A feature already in your repo at `mp2plus-base` is not scored again.\n113\t- **Every point is a line** with a measure and a check, listed under each exercise's **Scoring**. One flaw costs points in one line only.\n114\t\n115\t**How each exercise is laid out.** Every exercise has the same headings: Goal, Prerequisites, Roles, Steps, Prompt, Acceptance checklist, Out of scope, Deliverables, Report row, Scoring, If it goes wrong. The **Roles** line says which new routes are admin-only and which editors may use; S1 tests exactly those. The **Acceptance checklist** is the contract: the judge checks those steps and no others. Its **Checked by** column uses seven words. *walk*: the judge clicks through your app from a clean clone, at 1280 and 390 px. *publish*: the judge runs `uv run cms publish` and inspects `site/`. *test*: a named test for this step exists and passes. *read*: the judge reads the file or the code. *replay*: the judge sends the form's POST again without its CSRF token. *github*: the judge reads GitHub's own records and times (issues, pull requests, comments, pushes). *run*: the judge runs the command at the commit named.\n116\t\n117\t> **How a feature scores.** A feature worth P points has four lines. **Screenshots, 1:** both `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-{slug}-1280.png` and `-390.png` are present. **Trail, 1:** the issue existed before your first commit for it reached GitHub, and your findings comment came before the issue closed. **Tests, 1:** every step marked *test* has a named test that passes. **Function, P − 3:** floor((P − 3) × steps passed ÷ steps in the table). Process and security exercises score one point per checklist row, as listed in their tables.\n118\t\n119\t### The feature runbook\n120\tEvery feature's **Steps** follow MP2 Part 6's per-ticket loop. The commands, once:\n121\t\n122\t1. **The issue, before any code.** `gh issue create --label stretch --title \"MP2plus F2: Categories and tags\"`. When `gh` asks for the body, open the editor and paste the feature's acceptance table (the `| # | Step | Checked by |` rows) word for word, then a last line `Blocked by: none`. If `gh` says the label is not found, run `gh label create stretch` once and retry.\n123\t2. **The phase, before the session.** `echo T14 > .claude/state/phase`, with your issue number for 14. The usage ledger labels every turn with it, so set it before you start. Process and security exercises set their id instead, for example `echo S2 > .claude/state/phase`.\n124\t3. **The build.** `/clear`, then the feature's prompt with your issue number. `/implement` drives `/tdd`: a failing test first, then code.\n125\t4. **Your own walk.** Click through the acceptance checklist at 1280 and 390 px; fix what fails.\n126\t5. **Review, then close.** Send `/code-review`, then: `Post the code-review findings and how each was resolved as a comment on issue #14, then commit and push with a message that starts \"T14:\" and ends \"Closes #14\".`\n127\t6. **Check.** `gh issue view 14`. **Expected:** state CLOSED, with your findings comment above the close.\n128\t7. **Screenshots.** The feature working, at 1280 and 390 px, saved as named in its **Deliverables**.\n129\t8. **The two checks.** `uv run python scripts/check_submission.py --stage 2` and `uv run pytest -q`. Fix any failure that was not there at `mp2plus-base` before you start the next exercise.\n130\t\n131\tEvery feature prompt ends with the same rules; each one guards against a way to lose points. No `href=\"/` or `src=\"/` in any template, admin pages included: the MP2 checker greps every tracked `.html` file. A schema change needs a fresh database: `rm cms.db && uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py`. A new setting needs a line in `.env.example`, because the judge starts your app from it. Every new form needs a CSRF token.\n132\t\n133\t| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n134\t|---|---|---|\n135\t| Checker fails `no root-absolute paths in HTML` after a new admin page | a template has `href=\"/admin/...\"` | use `url_for(...)` or a relative link; the checker reads admin templates too |\n136\t| `sqlite3.OperationalError: no such column` | the schema changed; your old `cms.db` did not | `rm cms.db && uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py` |\n137\t| An MP2 test fails after your change | the feature changed behaviour an old test pins | add cases, never weaken old ones; if one assertion truly must change, declare it (Part 0 gate box) |\n138\t| Issue did not close | the commit message or PR body lacked `Closes #N` | `gh issue close 14 --comment \"closed by <sha>\"`; use the exact phrase next time |\n139\t| Checker fails on a transcript name | a file in `docs/transcripts/` breaks MP2's pattern | set `CMS_STUDENT=first-last` in `.env`; rename to `iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_{NN}_{first}-{last}_{YYYYMMDD}.md` |\n140\t| Your exercise has no row in `usage_report.py` | the phase was set after the session started | set it first next time; the ledger cannot be relabelled |\n141\t### 1.1 Features (F1–F9)\n142\t\n143\t#### F1 Media uploads with alt text (9 pts, about 45–60 min)\n144\t- **Goal.** Editors upload images to a media library and place them in posts. Every image carries alt text, so a screen reader can describe it.\n145\t- **Prerequisites.** MP2 done and Part 0 complete. Other exercises: none. Uses FastAPI's form uploads (`python-multipart` is already a dependency).\n146\t- **Roles.** Upload and media library: admin and editor.\n147\t\n148\t**Steps.**\n149\t1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F1: Media uploads with alt text`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n150\t2. `/clear`, then the prompt below with your issue number. Then walk the acceptance checklist yourself; fix what fails.\n151\t3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n152\t4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-media-1280.png` and `mp2plus-media-390.png`: the library with one image. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n153\t\n154\t**Prompt.**\n155\t```text\n156\t/implement #N Media uploads with alt text, for posts only. Follow the issue's acceptance checklist exactly. Store uploads under media/ and add media/ to .gitignore. Accept PNG and JPEG of 2 MB or less; check the type by extension and by the file's first bytes. The library shows a copyable Markdown snippet ![alt](media/<file>) for each item. cms publish copies media/ into site/media/ and makes every image src relative to the page that contains it. Rules: no href=\"/ or src=\"/ in any template, admin pages included; use url_for or relative links. Every new form carries a CSRF token and refuses a POST without it. Every new setting gets a line in .env.example. If you change the database schema, keep scripts/seed_demo.py running clean from .env.example and tell me to run rm cms.db && uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py. Tests that existed before may only gain cases; ask me before changing an existing assertion. Put the new tests in tests/test_media.py and name each one test_step<k>_<what> after the acceptance step it checks. Ask before adding any dependency.\n157\t```\n158\t**Acceptance checklist.**\n159\t| # | Step | Checked by |\n160\t|---|---|---|\n161\t| 1 | Upload a PNG or JPEG of 2 MB or less with alt text; it appears in the media library | walk, test |\n162\t| 2 | An upload with empty alt text is refused with a message | walk, test |\n163\t| 3 | A `.html` file, a `.html` file renamed to `.png`, and any file over 2 MB are refused: the type is checked by extension and by the file's first bytes | walk, test |\n164\t| 4 | Uploads are stored under `media/`, and `media/` is git-ignored | read |\n165\t| 5 | Each library item shows a copyable Markdown snippet, `![<alt text>](media/<file>)` | walk |\n166\t| 6 | The upload form carries a CSRF token; its POST replayed without the token is refused | replay, test |\n167\t| 7 | After `uv run cms publish`, the file is in `site/media/`, and the post's `<img>` has the alt text and a relative `src` that loads in the preview | publish, test |\n168\t\n169\t**The judge runs:** upload an image; try an empty alt text, a `.html` file and a `.html` file renamed to `.png`; paste a snippet into a published post; publish; preview with `python3 -m http.server -d site 8001`; check that every `<img>` in `site/` has `alt=` and loads.\n170\t- **Out of scope.** Inserting images with a button, resizing, thumbnails, deleting media, video, drag-and-drop, images in pages.\n171\t- **Deliverables.** The closed issue; `tests/test_media.py`; `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-media-1280.png` and `-390.png`; your report row.\n172\t- **Report row.** `| F1 | issue #<n>; tests/test_media.py; mp2plus-media-1280.png, mp2plus-media-390.png |`\n173\t- **Scoring.** Screenshots 1 · Trail 1 · Tests 1 · Function 6 = floor(6 × steps passed ÷ 7). Total 9.\n174\t\n175\t**If it goes wrong.**\n176\t| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n177\t|---|---|---|\n178\t| Images load on the home page, broken on post pages | a post page needs `../media/x.png` | make `src` relative to each page's folder |\n179\t| A renamed `.html` file is accepted | only the extension is checked | compare the first bytes with the PNG and JPEG signatures |\n180\t#### F2 Categories and tags (8 pts, about 40–55 min)\n181\t- **Goal.** Readers browse by topic. Each post has one category and any number of tags, and the public site has one index page per category and per tag.\n182\t- **Prerequisites.** MP2 done and Part 0 complete. Other exercises: none. A schema change: rebuild `cms.db` afterwards.\n183\t- **Roles.** Category and tag fields in the post editor, and the category filter: admin and editor.\n184\t\n185\t**Steps.**\n186\t1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F2: Categories and tags`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n187\t2. `/clear`, then the prompt below with your issue number. Then `rm cms.db && uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py`, then walk the acceptance checklist yourself; fix what fails.\n188\t3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n189\t4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-categories-1280.png` and `mp2plus-categories-390.png`: a published category page. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n190\t\n191\t**Prompt.**\n192\t```text\n193\t/implement #N Categories and tags, for posts only. Follow the issue's acceptance checklist exactly. One category per post as a single field; tags as one comma-separated field. The admin content list filters by category only. cms publish writes site/category/<slug>.html and site/tag/<slug>.html with published posts only, and every link between them is relative. Add the new terms to CONTEXT.md. Rules: no href=\"/ or src=\"/ in any template, admin pages included; use url_for or relative links. Every new form carries a CSRF token and refuses a POST without it. Every new setting gets a line in .env.example. If you change the database schema, keep scripts/seed_demo.py running clean from .env.example and tell me to run rm cms.db && uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py. Tests that existed before may only gain cases; ask me before changing an existing assertion. Put the new tests in tests/test_taxonomy.py and name each one test_step<k>_<what> after the acceptance step it checks. Ask before adding any dependency.\n194\t```\n195\t**Acceptance checklist.**\n196\t| # | Step | Checked by |\n197\t|---|---|---|\n198\t| 1 | A post has one category (a single field) and tags (one comma-separated field); both persist after save | walk, test |\n199\t| 2 | The admin content list filters by category | walk, test |\n200\t| 3 | `uv run cms publish` writes `site/category/<slug>.html` and `site/tag/<slug>.html`, each listing its published posts | publish, test |\n201\t| 4 | Each published post links to its category page and its tag pages with relative paths | publish |\n202\t| 5 | A draft with the same category and tag is absent from both index pages | publish, test |\n203\t\n204\t**The judge runs:** give two posts a category and tags; filter the admin list by category; publish; open a category page and a tag page from a post's links; add a draft with the same category and tag, publish again, and check it is absent.\n205\t- **Out of scope.** Nested categories, tag clouds, a tag filter in the admin list, categories on pages, renaming a tag across posts.\n206\t- **Deliverables.** The closed issue; `tests/test_taxonomy.py`; `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-categories-1280.png` and `-390.png`; your report row.\n207\t- **Report row.** `| F2 | issue #<n>; tests/test_taxonomy.py; mp2plus-categories-1280.png, mp2plus-categories-390.png |`\n208\t- **Scoring.** Screenshots 1 · Trail 1 · Tests 1 · Function 5 = floor(5 × steps passed ÷ 5). Total 8.\n209\t\n210\t**If it goes wrong.**\n211\t| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n212\t|---|---|---|\n213\t| Category links break from a post page | a post page needs `../category/x.html` | make links relative to each page's folder |\n214\t| A tag with a space makes a broken file name | no slug was generated | slugify tag and category names for file names |\n215\t#### F3 Revision history with rollback (8 pts, about 40–55 min)\n216\t- **Goal.** Nobody loses work to a bad edit. Every save of a post stores a revision, and rolling back copies an old revision forward as a new one, so the history only grows.\n217\t- **Prerequisites.** MP2 done and Part 0 complete. Other exercises: none. A schema change: rebuild `cms.db` afterwards.\n218\t- **Roles.** Revision list, revision view and rollback: admin and editor.\n219\t\n220\t**Steps.**\n221\t1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F3: Revision history with rollback`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n222\t2. `/clear`, then the prompt below with your issue number. Then `rm cms.db && uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py`, then walk the acceptance checklist yourself; fix what fails.\n223\t3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n224\t4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-revisions-1280.png` and `mp2plus-revisions-390.png`: a post's revision list. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n225\t\n226\t**Prompt.**\n227\t```text\n228\t/implement #N Revision history with rollback, for posts only. Follow the issue's acceptance checklist exactly. Store revisions in their own table, with who, when, title and body, written on every save including the first create. Rollback is a POST that creates a new revision; it never deletes or rewrites an old one. Rules: no href=\"/ or src=\"/ in any template, admin pages included; use url_for or relative links. Every new form carries a CSRF token and refuses a POST without it. Every new setting gets a line in .env.example. If you change the database schema, keep scripts/seed_demo.py running clean from .env.example and tell me to run rm cms.db && uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py. Tests that existed before may only gain cases; ask me before changing an existing assertion. Put the new tests in tests/test_revisions.py and name each one test_step<k>_<what> after the acceptance step it checks. Ask before adding any dependency.\n229\t```\n230\t**Acceptance checklist.**\n231\t| # | Step | Checked by |\n232\t|---|---|---|\n233\t| 1 | Every save of a post, the first create included, stores a revision with who, when, title and body | test |\n234\t| 2 | The admin lists a post's revisions, newest first, and shows any one of them | walk |\n235\t| 3 | Creating a post and saving it twice more lists three revisions | walk, test |\n236\t| 4 | Rolling back to revision 1 adds revision 4, whose title and body equal revision 1's; nothing is deleted | walk, test |\n237\t| 5 | The rollback form carries a CSRF token; its POST replayed without the token is refused | replay, test |\n238\t| 6 | Revisions survive a server restart | walk |\n239\t\n240\t**The judge runs:** create a post and save it twice more (three revisions); roll back to revision 1 (four revisions, revision 1's body); replay the rollback POST without its token; restart the server and check the list again.\n241\t- **Out of scope.** Revisions of pages, users or settings; side-by-side diffs; pruning; autosave.\n242\t- **Deliverables.** The closed issue; `tests/test_revisions.py`; `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-revisions-1280.png` and `-390.png`; your report row.\n243\t- **Report row.** `| F3 | issue #<n>; tests/test_revisions.py; mp2plus-revisions-1280.png, mp2plus-revisions-390.png |`\n244\t- **Scoring.** Screenshots 1 · Trail 1 · Tests 1 · Function 5 = floor(5 × steps passed ÷ 6). Total 8.\n245\t\n246\t**If it goes wrong.**\n247\t| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n248\t|---|---|---|\n249\t| Two revisions per save | both the create and the update path write one | write the revision in one shared save function |\n250\t| Rollback is a link, not a form | a GET changes state | make it a POST form with a CSRF token |\n251\t#### F4 Scheduled publishing (7 pts, about 35–50 min)\n252\t- **Goal.** An author finishes a post on Sunday and wants it public on Tuesday. The post gets a `publish_at` time and the status `scheduled`, and goes public the next time you publish after that time.\n253\t- **Prerequisites.** MP2 done and Part 0 complete. Other exercises: none. A new setting, `CMS_TIMEZONE` (default `America/New_York`), read with Python's `zoneinfo`; a schema change.\n254\t- **Roles.** Scheduling in the post editor: admin and editor.\n255\t\n256\t**Steps.**\n257\t1. Create the issue (runbook 1), title `MP2plus F4: Scheduled publishing`; then `echo T<issue#> > .claude/state/phase`.\n258\t2. `/clear`, then the prompt below with your issue number. Then `rm cms.db && uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py`, then walk the acceptance checklist yourself; fix what fails.\n259\t3. `/code-review`, then the close-out message (runbook 5). `gh issue view <issue#>` shows CLOSED, with your comment above the close.\n260\t4. Save `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-scheduled-1280.png` and `mp2plus-scheduled-390.png`: the admin list with a scheduled post. Then run the two checks (runbook 8).\n261\t\n262\t**Prompt.**\n263\t```text\n264\t/implement #N Scheduled publishing, for posts only. Follow the issue's acceptance checklist exactly. Add the status scheduled and a publish_at time. Read CMS_TIMEZONE with zoneinfo, default America/New_York, and add CMS_TIMEZONE=America/New_York to .env.example. Pass the current time into the publish filter instead of reading the clock inside it, so tests can freeze it. Rules: no href=\"/ or src=\"/ in any template, admin pages included; use url_for or relative links. Every new form carries a CSRF token and refuses a POST without it. Every new setting gets a line in .env.example. If you change the database schema, keep scripts/seed_demo.py running clean from .env.example and tell me to run rm cms.db && uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py. Tests that existed before may only gain cases; ask me before changing an existing assertion. Put the new tests in tests/test_scheduled.py and name each one test_step<k>_<what> after the acceptance step it checks. Ask before adding any dependency.\n265\t```\n266\t**Acceptance checklist.**\n267\t| # | Step | Checked by |\n268\t|---|---|---|\n269\t| 1 | A post can be saved with the status `scheduled` and a `publish_at` date and time | walk, test |\n270\t| 2 | The admin content list shows scheduled posts with their `publish_at` time | walk |\n271\t| 3 | `uv run cms publish` exports a scheduled post whose `publish_at` is now or earlier in `CMS_TIMEZONE` | publish, test |\n272\t| 4 | A scheduled post whose `publish_at` is in the future is not exported | publish, test |\n273\t| 5 | The tests freeze the clock: the current time is passed in, so they pass on any day | read, test |\n274\t| 6 | `.env.example` has the line `CMS_TIMEZONE=America/New_York`, and `America/New_York` is the default when the variable is unset | read, test |\n275\t\n276\t**The judge runs:** schedule one post an hour in the past and one a day ahead, both in `America/New_York` time; publish; check that only the first is in `site/` and that the admin list shows both with their times.\n277\t- **Out of scope.** A background scheduler, flipping the status to `published`, recurring posts, an unpublish time, scheduled pages.\n278\t- **Deliverables.** The closed issue; `tests/test_scheduled.py`; `docs/screenshots/mp2plus-scheduled-1280.png` and `-390.png`; your report row.\n279\t- **Report row.** `| F4 | issue #<n>; tests/test_scheduled.py; mp2plus-scheduled-1280.png, mp2plus-scheduled-390.png |`\n280\t- **Scoring.** Screenshots 1 · Trail 1 · Tests 1 · Function 4 = floor(4 × steps passed ÷ 6). Total 7.\n281\t\n282\t**If it goes wrong.**\n283\t| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n284\t|---|---|---|\n285\t| The test passes today, fails tomorrow | the code reads the real clock | pass `now` into the filter; tests pass a fixed time |\n286\t| A post goes public hours early or late | naive times compared with UTC | store aware times; compare in `CMS_TIMEZONE` |\n287\t#### F5 Site search (7 pts, about 35–50 min)\n288\t- **Goal.** Readers find a post without scrolling. `cms publish` writes a small search index and a `search.html` page that queries it with one short script.
