In your report, under `## P4`: paste the two lines, a cost table (one row per MP2plus phase, copied from the phase table: phase, turns, 5h spent, weekly), and two or three sentences on the largest gap between forecast and actual. Commit the report with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` (message `P4: after`) and push.\n\n**Prompt.** None: P4 is your own work.\n\n**Acceptance checklist.**\n| # | Step | Checked by | Pts |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | The \"before\" lines (`Tickets done` and `Forecast`) are in `notes/mp2plus-plan.md`, committed together with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` before the first commit of the exercise the plan names next | read | 1 |\n| 2 | The \"after\" lines are in the report, committed together with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` after at least one other exercise is done | read | 1 |\n| 3 | Re-running the same `usage_report.py` command at each of those two commits prints the same lines | run | 1 |\n| 4 | The report's cost table has one row per MP2plus phase, copied from the phase table of the \"after\" run | read, run | 1 |\n| 5 | Every exercise in your report has its own phase row in the \"after\" run (`T<issue#>` for a feature, its id otherwise) | run | 1 |\n| 6 | Two or three sentences explain the largest gap between the forecast cost per ticket and one exercise's actual cost, quoting both numbers and giving a cause | read | 1 |\n\n- **Out of scope.** Changing `usage_report.py`; dollar costs.\n- **Deliverables.** The `## P4 before` section in `notes/mp2plus-plan.md`; the `## P4` section in your report; both commits; your report row.\n- **Report row.** `| P4 | before: commit <sha>; after: commit <sha>; see the P4 section |`\n- **Scoring.** One point per row as listed. Total 6.\n\n**If it goes wrong.**\n| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n|---|---|---|\n| `NotImplementedError: MP2 Exercise B` | `spend()` was never written | finish MP2 Exercise B first (MP2 manual Part 5.6) |\n| Your exercise has no phase row | the phase was not set before the session | set it first next time; explain the gap in your sentences |\n### 1.3 Security (S1–S2)\n\n#### S1 Direct-URL access matrix (6 pts, about 30–45 min)\n- **Goal.** Checklist #10: access control lives in code, not in hidden links. One test asks every admin-only route, read from the app's router, whether the editor and an anonymous visitor are kept out. It extends MP2 rubric item D3.\n- **Prerequisites.** Part 0 complete. MP2's login and roles; `client_as` in `tests/conftest.py`. Do it after your features, so their admin-only routes are covered.\n- **Roles.** No new routes. The test covers every admin-only route, including those your features' Roles lines name.\n\n**Steps.**\n1. `echo S1 > .claude/state/phase`, then `/clear` and the prompt below.\n2. `uv run pytest -q tests/test_access_matrix.py`. **Expected:** `passed`.\n3. Check it yourself: add a throwaway admin-only route, marked the way your other admin routes are, and run the test again. It must cover the new route with no edit to the test. Delete the throwaway route.\n4. Commit with a message that starts `S1:` and push.\n\n**Prompt.**\n```text\n/tdd Write tests/test_access_matrix.py. Build the app with create_app() and collect every admin-only route from app.routes, using a marker in code (for example the admin-only dependency), not a hand-written list. For each route and its method, assert client_as(\"editor\") gets 403 or a redirect, and an anonymous client is redirected to login. Fill path parameters with the id of a seeded item. Do not change existing tests.\n```\n**Acceptance checklist.**\n| # | Step | Checked by | Pts |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | `tests/test_access_matrix.py` exists and passes | test | 1 |\n| 2 | Its route list is built from the app's router (`create_app().routes`) through a marker in code, such as the admin-only dependency, not typed by hand | read | 1 |\n| 3 | A new admin-only route, marked the same way, is tested without editing the test (the judge adds one in a scratch copy) | test | 1 |\n| 4 | Every admin-only route, feature routes included, is in the generated list | read, test | 1 |\n| 5 | For each route, the editor gets 403 or a redirect | test | 1 |\n| 6 | For each route, an anonymous client is redirected to login | test | 1 |\n\n- **Out of scope.** Testing editor routes, rate limits, CSRF.\n- **Deliverables.** `tests/test_access_matrix.py`; your report row.\n- **Report row.** `| S1 | tests/test_access_matrix.py |`\n- **Scoring.** One point per row as listed. Total 6. Rows 2 and 3 together are the \"generated from the router\" tier.\n\n**If it goes wrong.**\n| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n|---|---|---|\n| The test finds no routes | admin routes carry no marker the test can see | mark them all the same way, for example one shared dependency |\n| A route returns 422, not 403 | the path parameter is not a real id | fill it with a seeded item's id |\n#### S2 Login hardening (8 pts, about 40–55 min)\n- **Goal.** Two attacks, two fixes. Checklist #2: a wrong password and an unknown email give the same answer, so an attacker cannot learn which emails have accounts. And guessing is slowed: after five failed logins within 15 minutes, the next attempt is refused.\n- **Prerequisites.** Part 0 complete. MP2's login. Adding a fixture to `tests/conftest.py` counts as adding a case and does not break the gate.\n- **Roles.** No new routes.\n\n**Steps.**\n1. `echo S2 > .claude/state/phase`, then `/clear` and the first prompt below.\n2. `/clear`, then the second prompt below.\n3. `uv run pytest -q`. **Expected:** `passed`, with no MP2 test failing. Commit with a message that starts `S2:` and push.\n\n**Prompt.**\n```text\n/tdd Make a wrong password and an unknown email produce the same login error message and status code. Add tests/test_login_hardening.py with a test that asserts both responses match.\n```\n```text\n/tdd Rate-limit login, keyed on the account: after 5 failed attempts for one email within 15 minutes, refuse further attempts for that email with 429 or a lockout message, even with the right password. Pass the clock in so tests can freeze it. In tests/test_login_hardening.py, test that attempt 6 is refused, that a login works again once 15 minutes have passed, and that another email can still log in. Reset the limiter in a fixture in tests/conftest.py. No new dependencies.\n```\n**Acceptance checklist.**\n| # | Step | Checked by | Pts |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | A wrong password and an unknown email give the same message and the same status code | walk | 1 |\n| 2 | A test asserts that both responses match | test | 1 |\n| 3 | After 5 failed logins within 15 minutes, the 6th attempt is refused with 429 or a lockout message, even with the right password | walk | 2 |\n| 4 | A test with a frozen clock asserts that attempt 6 is refused | test | 1 |\n| 5 | A test with a frozen clock asserts that a login works again once 15 minutes have passed | test | 1 |\n| 6 | The limiter is keyed on the account: 5 failures for one email do not block another email's correct login from the same client | test | 1 |\n| 7 | A fixture in `tests/conftest.py` resets the limiter between tests, so the full suite stays green | read, test | 1 |\n\n- **Out of scope.** CAPTCHAs, email alerts, password rules, limiting by IP address alone.\n- **Deliverables.** `tests/test_login_hardening.py`; the fixture in `tests/conftest.py`; your report row.\n- **Report row.** `| S2 | tests/test_login_hardening.py; fixture <name> in tests/conftest.py |`\n- **Scoring.** One point per row as listed. Total 8.\n\n**If it goes wrong.**\n| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n|---|---|---|\n| Other tests start failing with 429 | the limiter's state is shared across tests | reset it in the conftest fixture |\n| An MP2 login test fails | it asserted the old message text | declare the change (Part 0 gate box) and assert the new shared message |\n\n---\n\n## Part 2 — Submit\n**Time: 20 min. No points, but required: no tag and email, no points.**\n\n**Before you tag.** Every exercise you did has its report row. Then run:\n\n```bash\nuv run python scripts/check_submission.py --stage 2\nuv run pytest -q\n```\n\n**Expected:** no `FAIL` line that was not in your plan's base results, and pytest ends in `passed`. A failing test you wrote for MP2plus costs that exercise's Tests point only; a failing test that existed at `mp2plus-base` costs everything.\n\n**Tag.**\n\n```bash\ngit tag mp2plus-final\ngit push origin mp2plus-final\n```\n\n**Expected:** `* [new tag] mp2plus-final -> mp2plus-final`.\n\n**Report.** `docs/iphs400_mp2plus-web-cms_report_{first}-{last}_{YYYYMMDD}.md`, committed before the tag: one table row per exercise (copy each exercise's \"Report row\"), then one sentence per exercise on anything left broken. P4 adds its second forecast and comparison here. An exercise without a row is not scored.\n\n```text\n| Id | Evidence |\n|----|----------|\n| F2 | issue #14; tests/test_taxonomy.py; mp2plus-categories-1280.png, mp2plus-categories-390.png |\n| P1 | PR #15 closing issue #14; carries F2 |\n| S2 | tests/test_login_hardening.py; fixture reset_limiter in tests/conftest.py |\n```\n\n**Email** the instructor by Tue Oct 20, 2026, 2:40 pm (no-penalty grace until Wed Oct 21, 2026, 2:40 pm). The due date is the expected date; the grace period carries no penalty.\n- **Subject:** `IPHS400 MP2plus - First Last` (any dash counts; the words must match)\n- **Body:** repo URL; Pages URL; tag `mp2plus-final`.\n- **Attach:** your report file.\n\n---\n\n## Appendix A — How you are graded (summary)\nPart 0 and Part 2 are required and worth 0 points. Each exercise carries its own points, 111 in all. Your points are summed, capped at +30, and added to your MP2 score; the sum may exceed 100. The gate applies to every exercise. Every point is a line under an exercise's **Scoring**, and every lost point comes back with a specific fix. One flaw costs points in one line only. {"type":"api-request-blob","sessionId":"79ce639f-d0fe-42ed-9b5f-a887bcf43513","hash":"25e6c6e2d326aca7733e7b50b109bedc8e34efc2d8904afb8d39fb834b6177b7","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_013etasG6tdRGxJz7QnB7zA1","type":"tool_result","content":"580\t- **Goal.** Forecast what your next exercises will cost in usage, then measure what they did cost and explain the gap.\n581\t- **Prerequisites.** Part 0 complete. MP2 Exercise B done: `spend()` in `scripts/usage_report.py` works and `tests/test_usage_report.py` passes. One other exercise to measure, with its phase set (runbook 2).\n582\t- **Roles.** No new routes.\n583\t\n584\t**Steps.**\n585\t1. Before your next exercise, in your terminal and not inside Claude (so the ledger does not change between the run and the commit): `uv run python scripts/usage_report.py --remaining 3`, where 3 stands for the number of exercises still in your plan. **Expected:** the phase table, then `Tickets done: ...` and `Forecast: 3 tickets need ~X% of the weekly cap; Y% remains → FITS` (or `TIGHT`, or `OVER BUDGET`).\n586\t2. Paste those two lines into `notes/mp2plus-plan.md` under `## P4 before`, name the exercise you do next, and commit the plan with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` (message `P4: before`). Push. If the verdict is not `FITS`, apply MP2's fixes in order (MP2 manual Part 5.6) or pick a smaller exercise.\n587\t3. After at least one other exercise is done, run the same command in your terminal with the number of exercises you have left.\n588\t4. In your report, under `## P4`: paste the two lines, a cost table (one row per MP2plus phase, copied from the phase table: phase, turns, 5h spent, weekly), and two or three sentences on the largest gap between forecast and actual. Commit the report with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` (message `P4: after`) and push.\n589\t\n590\t**Prompt.** None: P4 is your own work.\n591\t\n592\t**Acceptance checklist.**\n593\t| # | Step | Checked by | Pts |\n594\t|---|---|---|---|\n595\t| 1 | The \"before\" lines (`Tickets done` and `Forecast`) are in `notes/mp2plus-plan.md`, committed together with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` before the first commit of the exercise the plan names next | read | 1 |\n596\t| 2 | The \"after\" lines are in the report, committed together with `notes/usage-ledger.csv` after at least one other exercise is done | read | 1 |\n597\t| 3 | Re-running the same `usage_report.py` command at each of those two commits prints the same lines | run | 1 |\n598\t| 4 | The report's cost table has one row per MP2plus phase, copied from the phase table of the \"after\" run | read, run | 1 |\n599\t| 5 | Every exercise in your report has its own phase row in the \"after\" run (`T<issue#>` for a feature, its id otherwise) | run | 1 |\n600\t| 6 | Two or three sentences explain the largest gap between the forecast cost per ticket and one exercise's actual cost, quoting both numbers and giving a cause | read | 1 |\n601\t\n602\t- **Out of scope.** Changing `usage_report.py`; dollar costs.\n603\t- **Deliverables.** The `## P4 before` section in `notes/mp2plus-plan.md`; the `## P4` section in your report; both commits; your report row.\n604\t- **Report row.** `| P4 | before: commit <sha>; after: commit <sha>; see the P4 section |`\n605\t- **Scoring.** One point per row as listed. Total 6.\n606\t\n607\t**If it goes wrong.**\n608\t| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n609\t|---|---|---|\n610\t| `NotImplementedError: MP2 Exercise B` | `spend()` was never written | finish MP2 Exercise B first (MP2 manual Part 5.6) |\n611\t| Your exercise has no phase row | the phase was not set before the session | set it first next time; explain the gap in your sentences |\n612\t### 1.3 Security (S1–S2)\n613\t\n614\t#### S1 Direct-URL access matrix (6 pts, about 30–45 min)\n615\t- **Goal.** Checklist #10: access control lives in code, not in hidden links. One test asks every admin-only route, read from the app's router, whether the editor and an anonymous visitor are kept out. It extends MP2 rubric item D3.\n616\t- **Prerequisites.** Part 0 complete. MP2's login and roles; `client_as` in `tests/conftest.py`. Do it after your features, so their admin-only routes are covered.\n617\t- **Roles.** No new routes. The test covers every admin-only route, including those your features' Roles lines name.\n618\t\n619\t**Steps.**\n620\t1. `echo S1 > .claude/state/phase`, then `/clear` and the prompt below.\n621\t2. `uv run pytest -q tests/test_access_matrix.py`. **Expected:** `passed`.\n622\t3. Check it yourself: add a throwaway admin-only route, marked the way your other admin routes are, and run the test again. It must cover the new route with no edit to the test. Delete the throwaway route.\n623\t4. Commit with a message that starts `S1:` and push.\n624\t\n625\t**Prompt.**\n626\t```text\n627\t/tdd Write tests/test_access_matrix.py. Build the app with create_app() and collect every admin-only route from app.routes, using a marker in code (for example the admin-only dependency), not a hand-written list. For each route and its method, assert client_as(\"editor\") gets 403 or a redirect, and an anonymous client is redirected to login. Fill path parameters with the id of a seeded item. Do not change existing tests.\n628\t```\n629\t**Acceptance checklist.**\n630\t| # | Step | Checked by | Pts |\n631\t|---|---|---|---|\n632\t| 1 | `tests/test_access_matrix.py` exists and passes | test | 1 |\n633\t| 2 | Its route list is built from the app's router (`create_app().routes`) through a marker in code, such as the admin-only dependency, not typed by hand | read | 1 |\n634\t| 3 | A new admin-only route, marked the same way, is tested without editing the test (the judge adds one in a scratch copy) | test | 1 |\n635\t| 4 | Every admin-only route, feature routes included, is in the generated list | read, test | 1 |\n636\t| 5 | For each route, the editor gets 403 or a redirect | test | 1 |\n637\t| 6 | For each route, an anonymous client is redirected to login | test | 1 |\n638\t\n639\t- **Out of scope.** Testing editor routes, rate limits, CSRF.\n640\t- **Deliverables.** `tests/test_access_matrix.py`; your report row.\n641\t- **Report row.** `| S1 | tests/test_access_matrix.py |`\n642\t- **Scoring.** One point per row as listed. Total 6. Rows 2 and 3 together are the \"generated from the router\" tier.\n643\t\n644\t**If it goes wrong.**\n645\t| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n646\t|---|---|---|\n647\t| The test finds no routes | admin routes carry no marker the test can see | mark them all the same way, for example one shared dependency |\n648\t| A route returns 422, not 403 | the path parameter is not a real id | fill it with a seeded item's id |\n649\t#### S2 Login hardening (8 pts, about 40–55 min)\n650\t- **Goal.** Two attacks, two fixes. Checklist #2: a wrong password and an unknown email give the same answer, so an attacker cannot learn which emails have accounts. And guessing is slowed: after five failed logins within 15 minutes, the next attempt is refused.\n651\t- **Prerequisites.** Part 0 complete. MP2's login. Adding a fixture to `tests/conftest.py` counts as adding a case and does not break the gate.\n652\t- **Roles.** No new routes.\n653\t\n654\t**Steps.**\n655\t1. `echo S2 > .claude/state/phase`, then `/clear` and the first prompt below.\n656\t2. `/clear`, then the second prompt below.\n657\t3. `uv run pytest -q`. **Expected:** `passed`, with no MP2 test failing. Commit with a message that starts `S2:` and push.\n658\t\n659\t**Prompt.**\n660\t```text\n661\t/tdd Make a wrong password and an unknown email produce the same login error message and status code. Add tests/test_login_hardening.py with a test that asserts both responses match.\n662\t```\n663\t```text\n664\t/tdd Rate-limit login, keyed on the account: after 5 failed attempts for one email within 15 minutes, refuse further attempts for that email with 429 or a lockout message, even with the right password. Pass the clock in so tests can freeze it. In tests/test_login_hardening.py, test that attempt 6 is refused, that a login works again once 15 minutes have passed, and that another email can still log in. Reset the limiter in a fixture in tests/conftest.py. No new dependencies.\n665\t```\n666\t**Acceptance checklist.**\n667\t| # | Step | Checked by | Pts |\n668\t|---|---|---|---|\n669\t| 1 | A wrong password and an unknown email give the same message and the same status code | walk | 1 |\n670\t| 2 | A test asserts that both responses match | test | 1 |\n671\t| 3 | After 5 failed logins within 15 minutes, the 6th attempt is refused with 429 or a lockout message, even with the right password | walk | 2 |\n672\t| 4 | A test with a frozen clock asserts that attempt 6 is refused | test | 1 |\n673\t| 5 | A test with a frozen clock asserts that a login works again once 15 minutes have passed | test | 1 |\n674\t| 6 | The limiter is keyed on the account: 5 failures for one email do not block another email's correct login from the same client | test | 1 |\n675\t| 7 | A fixture in `tests/conftest.py` resets the limiter between tests, so the full suite stays green | read, test | 1 |\n676\t\n677\t- **Out of scope.** CAPTCHAs, email alerts, password rules, limiting by IP address alone.\n678\t- **Deliverables.** `tests/test_login_hardening.py`; the fixture in `tests/conftest.py`; your report row.\n679\t- **Report row.** `| S2 | tests/test_login_hardening.py; fixture <name> in tests/conftest.py |`\n680\t- **Scoring.** One point per row as listed. Total 8.\n681\t\n682\t**If it goes wrong.**\n683\t| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n684\t|---|---|---|\n685\t| Other tests start failing with 429 | the limiter's state is shared across tests | reset it in the conftest fixture |\n686\t| An MP2 login test fails | it asserted the old message text | declare the change (Part 0 gate box) and assert the new shared message |\n687\t\n688\t---\n689\t\n690\t## Part 2 — Submit\n691\t**Time: 20 min. No points, but required: no tag and email, no points.**\n692\t\n693\t**Before you tag.** Every exercise you did has its report row. Then run:\n694\t\n695\t```bash\n696\tuv run python scripts/check_submission.py --stage 2\n697\tuv run pytest -q\n698\t```\n699\t\n700\t**Expected:** no `FAIL` line that was not in your plan's base results, and pytest ends in `passed`. A failing test you wrote for MP2plus costs that exercise's Tests point only; a failing test that existed at `mp2plus-base` costs everything.\n701\t\n702\t**Tag.**\n703\t\n704\t```bash\n705\tgit tag mp2plus-final\n706\tgit push origin mp2plus-final\n707\t```\n708\t\n709\t**Expected:** `* [new tag] mp2plus-final -> mp2plus-final`.\n710\t\n711\t**Report.** `docs/iphs400_mp2plus-web-cms_report_{first}-{last}_{YYYYMMDD}.md`, committed before the tag: one table row per exercise (copy each exercise's \"Report row\"), then one sentence per exercise on anything left broken. P4 adds its second forecast and comparison here. An exercise without a row is not scored.\n712\t\n713\t```text\n714\t| Id | Evidence |\n715\t|----|----------|\n716\t| F2 | issue #14; tests/test_taxonomy.py; mp2plus-categories-1280.png, mp2plus-categories-390.png |\n717\t| P1 | PR #15 closing issue #14; carries F2 |\n718\t| S2 | tests/test_login_hardening.py; fixture reset_limiter in tests/conftest.py |\n719\t```\n720\t\n721\t**Email** the instructor by Tue Oct 20, 2026, 2:40 pm (no-penalty grace until Wed Oct 21, 2026, 2:40 pm). Each exercise carries its own points, 111 in all. Your points are summed, capped at +30, and added to your MP2 score; the sum may exceed 100. The gate applies to every exercise. Every point is a line under an exercise's **Scoring**, and every lost point comes back with a specific fix. One flaw costs points in one line only. The full rubric is `docs/mp2plus-grading-rubric_20261012.md` in the starter repo.\n730\t\n731\t## Appendix B — Where this content comes from\n732\tEverything in MP2plus was introduced in MP2 but not required, or scored only lightly. \"MP2 rubric\" is `docs/mp2-grading-rubric_20260922.md`; \"MP2 manual\" is `docs/manual_iphs400_mp2-web-cms_20260922.md`. start every test with a clean slate.\"\"\"\n    from app import login_limiter\n\n    login_limiter.reset()\n    yield\n    login_limiter.reset()\napp/posts.py:46:def slugify(title: str) -> str:\napp/posts.py-47-    slug = re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]+\", \"-\", title.lower()).strip(\"-\") or \"post\"\napp/posts.py-48-    return slug + \"-1\" if slug in RESERVED_SLUGS else slug\napp/posts.py-49-\napp/posts.py-50-\napp/posts.py-51-def _unique_slug(conn: sqlite3.Connection, title: str) -> str:\napp/posts.py-52-    base = slugify(title)\napp/posts.py-53-    slug, n = base, 1\napp/posts.py-54-    while conn.execute(\"SELECT 1 FROM posts WHERE slug = ?\", (slug,)).fetchone():\napp/posts.py-55-        n += 1\napp/posts.py-56-        slug = f\"{base}-{n}\"\napp/posts.py-57-    return slug\napp/posts.py-58-\n47afe5b S2: Test same login error for wrong password and unknown email\n\n tests/test_login_hardening.py | 10 ++++++++++\n 1 file changed, 10 insertions(+)\n9f5853d S2: Rate-limit login per account (5 failures / 15 min -> 429)\n\n app/login_limiter.py                               |  34 +++++\n app/routes/auth.py                                 |  10 +-\n ...2-cms_chat-session_17_aishik-biswas_20261021.md | 142 +++++++++++++++++++++\n notes/usage-ledger.csv                             |   1 +\n tests/conftest.py                                  |  10 ++\n tests/test_login_hardening.py                      |  29 +++++\n 6 files changed, 225 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)\n# IPHS 400 MP2 Report: A CMS for the Kenyon College Finance Club\n\n**Aishik Biswas · October 15, 2026**  \n**Repository:** [GitHub](https://github.com/iaishikb-rgb/iphs400-mp2-cms) · **Live site:** [Finance Club CMS](https://iaishikb-rgb.github.io/iphs400-mp2-cms/)\n\n## 1. What I built, and which decisions were mine\n\nI built a small CMS for the Kenyon College Finance Club, which I run with my two co-presidents, Lucas Flynn and Madeline Ha. Our current Wix site doesn't represent the club well. It lists no officers or meeting times, its \"Contact Us\" link goes back to the homepage, and a Wix ad banner sits across the top. An alum landing on it could reasonably think we'd shut down. Most of our real information goes out in club emails, so members end up digging through old messages just to find out that we meet every other week in Oden 110.\n\nThe CMS has a local admin console built with FastAPI and SQLite. Presidents and Officers sign in there and write Posts and Pages in Markdown, with a preview next to the editor. `cms publish` turns the Live content into a static site on GitHub Pages. Presidents are admins; Officers are editors. All seven required capabilities work, and the suite has more than 120 tests.\n\nThese grill decisions were mine. In most of them I changed or rejected what Claude recommended:\n\n- **No forced password change at first sign-in.** Claude proposed it. I said no: we have about eight officers, and a President sets each password face to face. Given the deadline, another screen wasn't worth it. I kept a two-hour idle sign-out, though, because we share a laptop at meetings.\n- **Only Presidents publish.** Officers write drafts. In the spring our three sector teams pitch to Kenyon alumni at William Blair, and nothing half-finished should go out under the club's name.\n- **Only Presidents manage Pages.** This came straight from my sixth WordPress field note, where an Editor could move the About page to the trash. An officer posting an announcement shouldn't be able to touch Leadership or the Analyst Program page.\n- **A simple \"Publish this?\" confirm.** Claude wanted a summary of changes before every publish run. If something wrong goes out, we unpublish and run it again.\n- **Teams are credited in the body text,** not in a separate team field. The byline is just whoever posted it.\n- **The next meeting is a hand-edited line on the Homepage.** One of us already updates the email reminder the night before, so updating the site is the same habit.","is_error":false}]}}

What I built, and which decisions were mine\n\nI built a small CMS for the Kenyon College Finance Club, which I run with my two co-presidents, Lucas Flynn and Madeline Ha. Our current Wix site doesn't represent the club well. It lists no officers or meeting times, its \"Contact Us\" link goes back to the homepage, and a Wix ad banner sits across the top. An alum landing on it could reasonably think we'd shut down. Most of our real information goes out in club emails, so members end up digging through old messages just to find out that we meet every other week in Oden 110.\n\nThe CMS has a local admin console built with FastAPI and SQLite. Presidents and Officers sign in there and write Posts and Pages in Markdown, with a preview next to the editor. `cms publish` turns the Live content into a static site on GitHub Pages. Presidents are admins; Officers are editors. All seven required capabilities work, and the suite has more than 120 tests.\n\nThese grill decisions were mine. In most of them I changed or rejected what Claude recommended:\n\n- **No forced password change at first sign-in.** Claude proposed it. I said no: we have about eight officers, and a President sets each password face to face. Given the deadline, another screen wasn't worth it. I kept a two-hour idle sign-out, though, because we share a laptop at meetings.\n- **Only Presidents publish.** Officers write drafts. In the spring our three sector teams pitch to Kenyon alumni at William Blair, and nothing half-finished should go out under the club's name.\n- **Only Presidents manage Pages.** This came straight from my sixth WordPress field note, where an Editor could move the About page to the trash. An officer posting an announcement shouldn't be able to touch Leadership or the Analyst Program page.\n- **A simple \"Publish this?\" confirm.** Claude wanted a summary of changes before every publish run. If something wrong goes out, we unpublish and run it again.\n- **Teams are credited in the body text,** not in a separate team field. The byline is just whoever posted it.\n- **The next meeting is a hand-edited line on the Homepage.** One of us already updates the email reminder the night before, so updating the site is the same habit.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"48276fae-f0da-4863-b178-b55cbfe00bf4","permissionDecision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reasonType":"mode"},"session_id":"79ce639f-d0fe-42ed-9b5f-a887bcf43513","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/biswas1/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"79ce639f-d0fe-42ed-9b5f-a887bcf43513","version":"2.1.306","gitBranch":"main"}

tests/test_taxonomy.py; mp2plus-categories-1280.png, mp2plus-categories-390.png |\n| P1 | PR #12 closing issue #11; carries F2 |\n| S2 | tests/test_login_hardening.py; fixture reset_login_limiter in tests/conftest.py |\n| P3 | CONTEXT.md Decisions table; docs/adr/ADR-003-public-url-scheme.md |\n| P4 | before: commit eb10c3a; after: the commit \"P4: after\" that adds the P4 section; see the P4 section |\n\n## Left broken or worth knowing\n\n- **F2:** Category and tag names with no ASCII letters or digits (for example, non-Latin scripts) get no page of their own, because the slug function keeps only `a-z0-9`.\n- **P1:** The PR (#12) carried F2 only, no MP2 repair, and its body is just `Closes #11`.\n- **S2:** Giving a wrong password and an unknown email the same message already worked from MP2 T01, so that test only pins existing behaviour and the per-account rate limiter (5 failures in 15 minutes, then 429) is the only new work.\n- **P3:** ADR-003 records the public URL scheme; the Decisions table in CONTEXT.md holds the other decisions.\n- **P4:** The \"after\" commit does not exist yet, so the row names it by message instead of by sha.\n\n## P4\n\nAFTER_LINES_HERE\n\nCOST_TABLE_HERE\n\nGAP_SENTENCES_HERE\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2002,"cache_read_input_tokens":73681,"output_tokens":827,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":70},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2002,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":827,"cache_read_input_tokens":73681,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2002,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2002},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_01GLUVSTVt2MkuWGcyR6WvVT":{"file_path":"/Users/biswas1/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/docs/iphs400_mp2plus-web-cms_report_aishik-biswas_20261021.md","content":"# IPHS 400 MP2plus Report: Kenyon College Finance Club CMS\n\nAishik Biswas · October 21, 2026 · MP2plus extension of the Kenyon College Finance Club CMS (F2, P1, S2, P3, P4).\n\n## Evidence\n\n| Id | Evidence |\n|----|----------|\n| F2 | issue #11; tests/test_taxonomy.py; mp2plus-categories-1280.png, mp2plus-categories-390.png |\n| P1 | PR #12 closing issue #11; carries F2 |\n| S2 | tests/test_login_hardening.py; fixture reset_login_limiter in tests/conftest.py |\n| P3 | CONTEXT.md Decisions table; docs/adr/ADR-003-public-url-scheme.md |\n| P4 | before: commit eb10c3a; after: the commit \"P4: after\" that adds the P4 section; see the P4 section |\n\n## Left broken or worth knowing\n\n- **F2:** Category and tag names with no ASCII letters or digits (for example, non-Latin scripts) get no page of their own, because the slug function keeps only `a-z0-9`.\n- **P1:** The PR (#12) carried F2 only, no MP2 repair, and its body is just `Closes #11`.\n- **S2:** Giving a wrong password and an unknown email the same message already worked from MP2 T01, so that test only pins existing behaviour and the per-account rate limiter (5 failures in 15 minutes, then 429) is the only new work.\n- **P3:** ADR-003 records the public URL scheme; the Decisions table in CONTEXT.md holds the other decisions.\n- **P4:** The \"after\" commit does not exist yet, so the row names it by message instead of by sha.\n\n## P4\n\nAFTER_LINES_HERE\n\nCOST_TABLE_HERE\n\nGAP_SENTENCES_HERE\n"}},"apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CgFwSwvsWDPXo7jX3ZPFk","requestRef":"b1efffab-0f42-4a95-8a4a-2635bc97afda","requestedModel":"claude-sonnet-5-5","type":"assistant","uuid":"176cd63b-f76d-4c26-9676-54381559e2c9","timestamp":"2026-10-21T19:32:17.664Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"79ce639f-d0fe-42ed-9b5f-a887bcf43513","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/biswas1/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"79ce639f-d0fe-42ed-9b5f-a887bcf43513","version":"2.1.306","gitBranch":"main"}
